Player Ratings: Blackburn 2-1 West Ham as Hermansen Has a Nightmare

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

West Ham’s promising start at Ewood Park ultimately counted for very little as Scott Parker’s side slipped to a 2-1 defeat against Blackburn Rovers. Despite enjoying plenty of possession and creating opportunities, the Hammers were undone by poor defending and some particularly questionable goalkeeping.

Joël Piroe’s late goal gave West Ham a chance of rescuing something, but the longer the game went on, the worse we seemed to play. There were some decent individual performances, particularly early on, but others will want to forget this afternoon in a hurry.

A really poor afternoon for the goalkeeper. I thought he could have been far more commanding for Blackburn’s opening goal, whether that meant catching the cross, punching it or simply getting something on the ball. He did make a straightforward late save from Todd Cantwell, but that doesn’t change my overall assessment.

One of West Ham’s more reliable performers before being substituted. Made some useful defensive interceptions and regularly pushed forward to support Bowen down the right. Tried a couple of long throws into the penalty area, although they didn’t really cause Blackburn much trouble.

Had some positive moments going forward, including a driving run from defence and a header that went narrowly wide early in the second half. However, I thought he could have done better for Blackburn’s first goal, and his performance deteriorated badly towards the end. Was fortunate not to concede a penalty before slipping twice during a dreadful passage of play that handed Blackburn a dangerous counter-attack.

Probably West Ham’s most dependable defender on the day. Made several important interceptions, particularly down the left where Blackburn repeatedly threatened, and was willing to carry the ball forward to help build attacks. Also produced a decent cross for Castellanos, although the resulting header went wide.

A mixed afternoon for the youngster. I was impressed with his willingness to get forward, his passing combinations with Austbø and some of the crosses he delivered. However, Blackburn repeatedly targeted his side, often leaving him facing two opponents, and he was beaten too easily in the build-up to the opening goal. Still very much learning the position.

Didn’t think he had a particularly good game. Had plenty of involvement, took the corners and got himself into shooting positions, but too often the final product was disappointing. Lost possession unnecessarily on occasions, failed to close down Blackburn’s second goalscorer quickly enough and wasted a promising late free kick with poor delivery.

You certainly couldn’t fault his work rate. At times he seemed to be everywhere, covering at left-back, appearing on the right and getting forward to support the attack. However, he wasn’t nearly as influential on the ball as he had been during the second half against Cardiff, and his shooting was disappointing. Replaced by Castellanos after 64 minutes.

One of West Ham’s more inventive players, particularly during the first half. Looked to move the ball quickly, attempted some clever through passes and wasn’t afraid to shoot, forcing one save and seeing another effort deflected behind. Not everything worked, but at least he was trying to make things happen.

I was genuinely surprised when he was substituted because he’d been one of West Ham’s livelier attacking players. Produced a lovely dribble past two defenders in the first half, linked up well with Mayers and delivered an excellent cross that Bowen headed against the post. Could perhaps have offered Mayers more defensive protection before Blackburn’s opener, but I thought he was contributing plenty going forward.

Blackburn 2-1 West Ham: Austbo was one of the Hammers better players

Worked hard to make things happen and was heavily involved in West Ham’s attacking play, particularly from the right. Unlucky to see his header strike the post following Austbø’s cross, while a difficult chance from close range went wide as he was falling and sliding. Also produced a lovely chipped delivery for Souček’s late header, but couldn’t quite provide the decisive contribution West Ham needed.

A frustrating performance rescued to some extent by an excellent goal. Too often he seemed hesitant in promising positions, allowing defenders to recover, while one particularly poor pass in the second half wasted a good attacking opportunity. However, he reacted brilliantly to Castellanos’ cross in the 82nd minute, firing high into the roof of the net. Whatever else you say about his performance, he took that chance extremely well.

Replaced Austbø after 58 minutes and was practically anonymous. Considering how involved Austbø had been, the substitution certainly didn’t improve West Ham’s attacking threat down the left.

Introduced for Kanté after 64 minutes and provided the cross that led to Piroe’s goal. Also had a header go wide from a Kilman delivery and was involved in the scramble that presented Bowen with a difficult chance. At least contributed something tangible to the attack.

Didn’t have a great deal of time to influence proceedings, particularly with West Ham having already lost their shape. Played one very silly ball into Orford inside our own penalty area, creating unnecessary danger. However, he also got into one of those familiar goalscoring positions in stoppage time, only to direct his header straight at the goalkeeper from Bowen’s excellent delivery.

Another substitute who came into a team that had lost its shape and was struggling for confidence. Was put in a difficult position by Souček’s poor pass inside the penalty area and was fortunate not to concede a penalty. Difficult to judge him too harshly given the limited time he had on the pitch and the state of the game when he entered.

Apple Completes the Set: Every Magnificent Seven Stock Opts Out of OKX-ICE Venue

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Apple Completes the Set: Every Magnificent Seven Stock Opts Out of OKX-ICE Venue

Every member of Wall Street’s “Magnificent Seven” now wants its stock kept off OKX and ICE’s planned tokenized stock venue.

Apple and Meta Platforms are among 11 companies added to the list of objecting issuers in an updated notice from OKXICE TSV dated Thursday.

They join Microsoft, Alphabet, Amazon, Nvidia and Tesla, completing a sweep by the group of tech giants that makes up a large share of the S&P 500’s value. The other new objectors are Home Depot, Boeing, GE Vernova, Palantir, ServiceNow, S&P Global, KLA, Super Micro Computer and Nebius Group, bringing the total number of companies saying “no” to the OKX and ICE platform to 67.

Meta’s objection carries some irony. The Facebook parent once tried to launch its own stablecoin, Libra, later renamed Diem, before abandoning the project in 2022 amid regulatory pushback.

Apple had been one of the last megacaps missing from the list after Amazon, Tesla, and SpaceX objected in the venue’s Nov. 2 notice. The tally stood at 56 then, and at nine on Oct. 15, when the first objectors appeared.

Just as notable is who hasn’t objected. Crypto-friendly public companies such as Coinbase, Robinhood and Strategy don’t appear on the list. Coinbase launched tokenized stocks for non-U.S. users on its Base network in August. Berkshire Hathaway, Visa, and Exxon Mobil are also absent. It’s unclear whether OKXICE notified those companies.

Under the SEC’s innovation exemption, which the agency unveiled in September after the Clarity Act failed to advance in the Senate, a venue must notify a company before offering a version of its shares tokenized by an unaffiliated third party. If the company objects in writing within 30 days, the venue can’t make that stock available for trading, according to the SEC’s order. It’s worth noting, though, that the objection applies only to that venue.

OKXICE TSV, a 50-50 joint venture between NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange and OKX’s U.S. holding company, plans to offer tokenized U.S. stocks 24/7 through permissioned pools on OKX’s XLayer blockchain. An outside “tokenizer” would hold real shares one-for-one through a registered broker-dealer.

With the market’s biggest names opted out, it’s unclear which major stocks the venue will be able to offer at launch, and its notices don’t list the tokens it plans to make available.

Art, philosophy, and science blend in beautiful wildlife photography

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Published Nov 7, 2026 5:01 PM EST

Excerpted from UNITED NATURE by Andrew Zuckerman. Copyright © 2026 by Andrew Zuckerman. Published in the United States by Crown, an imprint of the Crown Publishing Group, a division of Penguin Random House LLC.

For as long as I can remember, I have made images. Much of my childhood was spent in the darkroom in my parents’ basement, surrounded by the smell of chemicals and the magic of forms slowly appearing on paper. Of all the things I’ve pursued in my life, the camera has been the one constant. It has shaped how I see: a device that slows me down, compels me to look more deeply and to understand the subjects on an essential level. And for me, the subject has always been other forms of life.

In that sense, I am part of a long tradition. The very first images humans made were not of themselves, but of other beings. Almost forty thousand years ago, in the caves of Chauvet in France, someone painted lions, rhinoceroses, mammoths, and bears with striking naturalism, allowing the animals to emerge from the contours and relief of the stone walls. Nearly twenty thousand years later, in the caves of Lascaux, we find more painted walls: herds of horses in motion and bison colliding, painted with sophisticated perspective and composition. Humans have never stopped depicting other beings. In Egypt, cats and jackals accompanied the dead into eternity. Dolphins appear again and again on Greek pottery. In medieval manuscripts, we find lions and owls used to represent human virtues and vices. Enlightenment engravers focused on the natural world, seeking order through detail. A generation later, John James Audubon’s The Birds of America presented perhaps the most realistic natural history illustration: life-size depictions of herons, warblers, and owls, rendered with exquisite precision.

With the arrival of photography two centuries ago, the human act of depicting the natural world shifted profoundly. This was not just a new tool, it was a rupture: the first time nature could enact itself with a fidelity and presence previously unseen. The world’s first published book of photographs arrived in 1843: Photographs of British Algae: Cyanotype Impressions by Anna Atkins. Atkins treated paper with a chemical solution, added specimens onto the treated paper, and left them under sunlight. This produced a white silhouette against a blue background. Atkins created these pictures of algae at the height of botanical illustration. While her peers were exhibiting artistic mastery over nature, she was removing herself. Here, the algae left its own impression directly on the paper. The plant depicted itself. The plant had agency.

One year after Atkins’s algae cyanotypes, William Henry Fox Talbot published The Pencil of Nature, a book containing twenty-four calotypes, the first paper negative, which allowed multiple positive prints to be made from a single negative. Photography was so new that Talbot felt required to include a notice: “The plates of the present work are impressed by the agency of Light alone, without any aid whatsoever from the artist’s pencil. They are the sun-pictures themselves, and not, as some persons have imagined, engravings in imitation.” Like Atkins, Talbot emphasized that the image was not mediated by the hand of the artist but drawn directly by nature itself—“sun-pictures” made by light. These early inventors of photography were introducing a new way of observing and recording the living world—one in which nature was no longer simply depicted, but allowed to speak for itself.

Only a few years later, in 1859, Charles Darwin published On the Origin of Species, advancing the radical idea that all life was in flux and that variation, adaptation, and transformation were the fundamental conditions of existence. In this light, Atkins’s cyanotypes seem almost prophetic: life depicted not as a fixed emblem to be mastered, but as a living process.

Skipping forward to the late nineteenth century, Cherry Kearton was pioneering what is now considered wildlife photography by hiding in hollowed-out ox carcasses or building bird-shaped blinds to get close to subjects. In the early twentieth century, photography extended into motion. Eadweard Muybridge’s studies of horses and birds revealed the invisible mechanics of gait and flight, moments too fast for the human eye to clock. Jacques Cousteau created ocean documentaries that gave the general population access to and intimacy with distant species.

By the late twentieth century, nature photography and film had become not only a way of seeing, but also a way of caring—shaping public consciousness about endangered species, vanishing habitats, and the fragility of ecosystems.

This notion of the image as a tool to influence our thinking found perhaps its most profound expression in the late 1960s, when Stewart Brand lobbied NASA to release the first photographs of the whole Earth seen from space. When those images finally appeared on the cover of his Whole Earth Catalog, they offered a new planetary perspective: not a map or an illustration, but the living sphere itself. For the first time, humanity could see Earth as a singular organism.

That one image—blue oceans swirling with white clouds, landmasses without borders—reshaped the cultural imagination. It gave rise to the environmental movement, inspired new sciences of systems and ecology, and embedded the idea that the planet itself is alive into the culture.

In the decades that followed, technology expanded the reach of our seeing. Satellite imagery made the whole Earth continuously observable: forests shrinking, glaciers collapsing, storms forming in real time. Microscopy revealed networks of neurons firing, cells dividing, viruses mutating. Telescopes peered to the edges of the universe, returning images of galaxies colliding and stars being born. Today, cameras can show bacteria and galaxies with equal fidelity.

Each age has found its own surface, its own technique, but the effort has always been an attempt at understanding both the lives of others and our own experience. Across this long arc, from the walls of Chauvet to the cyanotypes of Atkins, from Muybridge’s horses to Brand’s Whole Earth, images of the living world have not only recorded what is here, but affected how we think and feel about it. They remind us that seeing is never neutral: It is always an act of framing, and an invitation to imagine our place within the larger weave of existence.

The frame itself is an extraordinary tool of delineation. As an editor, it decides what belongs and what does not. The power of the frame lies in its capacity to determine which information matters.

My focus then started to come out in how I made pictures. The subject itself mattered less to me than resolving how to present it. Eventually, I found the pure white background, where the image becomes an encounter: a relation between two living beings shaped by a shared evolutionary history.

The white background was never an aesthetic choice. It’s purely functional. We never look at something singularly, but rather are simultaneously looking at the subject in relation to ourselves. I’ve often felt that our sense of ourselves is not a figure, it’s an abstraction. We don’t think of ourselves as a figure in relation to the ground. And so when wanting to provide the opportunity of connection to the subject—the other—that subject presented on white creates a shared plane for it and us. White space isn’t empty. It’s a condition that allows perception to happen. It lets the image reach the body before it’s interpreted by the mind. In that space, assumptions can fall away and the subject can be encountered in a preconceptual field, before meaning has settled.

2026 Popular Science Home of the Future Awards

39 products that will improve your everyday life.

Artist and creative director Andrew Zuckerman has spent 30 years traveling the world capturing the alluring and spectacular world of nature. Known for creating hyper-real images set against stark backgrounds, his artworks have been exhibited and acquired by public institutions and private collections throughout the world, and his books have been published in several international editions. Alongside his artistic practice, he has advised and led creative work for pioneering companies in technology, climate innovation, and energy, including Apple and Google. He lives in New York City with his wife and three children.

What Couples Married For 40 Years Do When Life Gets Hard

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Couples married for decades reveal what they turned to when they thought their marriage might end.

Couples married for decades reveal what they turned to when they thought their marriage might end.

When people in decades-long marriages describe what helped them through a crisis, most of the responses they give involve leaning on each other.

They describe listening to each other, talking through painful subjects and sometimes having the same argument more than once.

The researchers suggest that this response grows out of years of knowing they can count on their partner.

In attachment theory, a partner can act as a safe haven, someone whose presence makes it easier to bear distress.

Getting through a difficult period together may deepen that trust, making it easier to ask for help the next time it is needed.

Dr Christian Heim, the study’s first author, and Dr Caroline Heim, study co-author, interviewed each couple together, asking each person to describe what had put their marriage under strain and what had helped them cope.

The 180 participants were born in 24 countries and, with a few exceptions, had been married for at least 40 years.

Around two-thirds of the couples described a threat they believed could have ended their marriage, such as the death or serious illness of a child, infidelity or prolonged periods apart.

Talking, listening and arguing accounted for almost a quarter of the coping responses the researchers recorded.

After an affair, some couples described arguing over it repeatedly, while bereaved partners sometimes waited for a moment when both felt able to speak.

One partner in an American couple married for 45 years recalled asking their spouse about the son they had lost.

“Can I talk about our son? I’m hurting. Is it okay to talk about it?”

Some partners described taking time to grieve separately before they were ready to turn to each other.

Around four in five coping responses involved leaning on the relationship, whether by drawing closer, persevering as a couple or giving up something one partner wanted.

The interviews capture what couples in lasting marriages felt had helped them, leaving open whether the same approaches would have helped couples who separated.

When partners see a problem as something they face together, they may spend less time working out who is to blame and more time trying to understand what the other person is going through.

A previous analysis involving almost 18,000 people found that coping together and helping a partner manage stress were both linked to greater relationship satisfaction (Falconier et al., 2015).

Some couples in the interviews felt that an illness, a bereavement or a family conflict had left them closer, even though the experience itself had been painful.

A British couple married for 60 years described how they got through a serious conflict with their in-laws:

The study was published in the journal The American Journal of Family Therapy (Heim & Heim, 2025).

Psychologist, Jeremy Dean, PhD is the founder and author of PsyBlog. He holds a doctorate in psychology from University College London and two other advanced degrees in psychology. He has been writing about scientific research on PsyBlog since 2004. View all posts by Dr Jeremy Dean

Muniz rescues late point for Fulham against Newcastle after Murphy stunner

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Questions over whether Álvaro Arbeloa and Matthias Jaissle have what it takes to succeed in the Premier League remain unanswered after these two young foreign managers watched their sides grind out a 1-1 draw at Craven Cottage.

Arbeloa’s Fulham lacked fizz, physicality and personality before rescuing a point thanks to a late equaliser from Rodrigo Muniz, while Newcastle displayed little of Jaissle’s high-octane philosophy and squandered the chance to move into the top half after failing to build on a vicious opening goal from Jacob Murphy.

A second consecutive away win in the league was on the cards when Murphy seized on an error from César Palacios and scored in the 80th minute. Jaissle has faced criticism over his tactics and training methods since replacing Eddie Howe just before the start of the season but the German was happy with elements of this Newcastle performance.

The 38-year-old saw “a complete commitment and togetherness in all phases of the game” and was entitled to argue the visitors had the better chances, an impressive feat given there was such little threat from Jaissle’s front two of William Osula and Yoane Wissa.

It remains a big problem for Newcastle, who are paying for their failure to fill the void left by Alexander Isak’s move to Liverpool last year. They witnessed another half-hearted display from Wissa before the £55m striker’s withdrawal in the 61st minute and were hindered by the bluntness of Osula, whose hopes of grabbing a rare goal were somewhat hindered by his failure to register a touch in Fulham’s area.

To make matters worse Osula was also partially responsible for the leveller. Newcastle needed the 23-year-old to hold the ball up but he was outmuscled near the halfway line, allowing Fulham to pounce. Palacios, who had given the ball away for Murphy’s goal, wriggled into the area and combined with another of Arbeloa’s substitutes by smuggling the ball across for Muniz to score his first league goal since August 2025.

Jaissle felt it was a “slapstick” concession. Arbeloa, who likes to play an expansive style of passing football, focused more on Muniz’s impact after the target man’s introduction in place of the smaller Gonzalo García. “Newcastle were marking us man to man,” Fulham’s Spanish head coach said. “They forced us to play long balls and Rodrigo is very strong in this situation. He got his chance. It is very important for us because I need a player like Rodrigo. He can score goals but create so many chances.”

It is encouraging that Arbeloa was willing to be flexible and more direct. However this was a poor game between two sides too close to the bottom three for comfort. Fulham also fretted over Josh King after the 19-year-old went off with a groin injury. The attacking midfielder is set for a scan and will hope not to have suffered any significant damage after earning his first England call-up on Friday.

Thomas Tuchel will be keen to have King available. Newcastle paid a lot of attention to the teenager, with Sean Steur and Nico González sitting deep in midfield, but they could not keep him quiet during the first half. King tested Lukas Hornicek with a volley from 20 yards and later missed a decent headed chance.

Fulham were not at their best. Oscar Bobb and García went close after an error from Steur but Newcastle were comfortable. Jaissle, who made four changes after accusing his players of not following instructions during last Monday’s defeat by Everton, asked his side to play a patient game and was annoyed when they had a goal ruled out in the 33rd minute. Dan Burn did nothing wrong when he converted a cross from close range, but Wissa was penalised for a holding offence on Timothy Castagne off the ball.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

Jaissle called it a soft decision. Newcastle, who saw Bernd Leno twice thwart Harvey Barnes before half-time, moved on and improved in the second half. They pressed with more intensity and created opportunities for Barnes and Wissa.

Fulham had no out ball with García unable to impose himself against Burn and Malick Thiaw. Palacios came on and was at fault when Newcastle broke the deadlock. The Spaniard played an aimless pass across his own half and was punished by Murphy, who seized possession before driving forward to cut inside and crack a brilliant shot past Leno from 25 yards.

It seemed Newcastle were about to ease the pressure on Jaissle. Fulham had other ideas. Arbeloa praised the defiance. Palacios linked with Alex Iwobi and found Muniz. The ball bounced around the six-yard box and Muniz forced it past Hornicek.

Fulham poured forward in search of a winner. Lewis Hall almost restored Newcastle’s lead, only for Leno to deny the left-back. A draw was fair.

How Modders Spent Three Years Remastering Mass Effect 3’s Fantastic Multiplayer For Legendary Edition: ‘Let’s Do Something Stupid’

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Mass Effect 3Mass Effect: Legendary EditionMass Effect: UniverseN7 Day

When the Mass Effect: Legendary Edition remasters launched in 2021, they had just about everything you could have wanted from a modern-day port of BioWare’s sci-fi RPGs. There were, however, two major exemptions: the original game’s Pinnacle Station DLC, and Mass Effect 3’s cooperative multiplayer mode. In 2021, the ME3Tweaks modding group brought back Pinnacle Station. Now, five years later, it’s back to finish the job, and is releasing a remastered mod of the multiplayer mode for those who have the Legendary Edition on PC.

The project began in earnest three years ago, with modder Mgamerz coming up with the idea shortly after wrapping up a randomizer mod for the Mass Effect 2 remaster. Luckily, the ME3Tweaks group had a lot of the foundations set in place to bring the missing mode into the Legendary Edition after years of working in the Mass Effect modding space.

“There was somebody who had shown that they could port a level when they found an unused level in [Mass Effect 1’s remaster],” he tells Kotaku. “There was like an extra Citadel area, and I was like, ‘Oh man, what if I could port that to another game?’ Sometimes I just get these crazy ideas, like, ‘What if I just did something crazy?’ And so I tried porting it, and I was able to actually get it to port because we did Pinnacle Station, so we had a lot of the infrastructure to do it, and I was able to port it. I’m like, ‘man, what if we could do like a multiplayer level?’ And I was able to port, I think it was [Firebase] Condor. It’s one of the first DLC maps. It  looked terrible, but you didn’t immediately crash the game loading into it. And I had already ported multiplayer levels before in my Redemption map pack that I had made from single player to multiplayer, so I kind of knew everything that was needed. And then it was like, ‘Okay, well, I made this map, but there’s no game mode; you can’t do anything on it. Really, it looks terrible.’ And so I kind of shelved it for a couple of months.”

After some time, Mgamerz decided he wanted to make a multiplayer mod, and thus went back to the original Mass Effect 3, and says he was struck by how much “further advanced” the group’s modding tools had become while working on the remaster.

“Literally probably about three hours into it, I’m like, ‘These tools suck,’ and I say that as someone who helped write most of them. Our tooling for the original trilogy is so bad compared to what we got in Legendary Edition.”

Part of that is because, prior to the remaster’s launch, the ME3Tweaks group had meetings with folks at BioWare about making the Legendary Edition more modder-friendly, and although the company wasn’t able to implement everything, it did give the group a better starting point.

“It was probably just like a month before the game release,” he recalls. “We gave them a list of some of the most awful things we have to deal with, and we kind of didn’t expect them to do most of the changes. And they didn’t. I mean, it’s one month before launch, so you can’t really make big changes. But they snuck a few in right at the end of development for us, which has helped a lot of single-player development. They gave us some information that we couldn’t really leverage until we started working on stuff like this, like how does DLC mount work? When the game loads, DLC mounts into the game, but in ME3, it loads single-player versus multiplayer, and we didn’t really have any idea how any of that worked. So they gave us information about that, and then we, you know, over the years we built on top of it.”

Over two years later, on November 7, 2023, Mgamerz created a Discord server for Project Milan, what would become the Mass Effect 3 Legendary Edition multiplayer remaster mod, telling the rest of the ME3Tweaks modders, “Let’s do something stupid. Let’s port multiplayer.”

He says that development was on and off over the course of three years, citing some personal matters that got in the way including breaking his arm at some point during the whole effort, but the group had a working prototype of the mode as early as December of 2023.

“You could pick at least one kit, and the game didn’t crash until you got to wave three,” he recalls. “Enemies would spawn. Audio was totally broken. Like there was virtually no audio. The shaders were all broken, so you just got checkerboards all over the place. But you could actually play it, and enemies spawn. You could kill them. You get points. You know, the absolute bare bones were there.”

From there, the team built the rest of the multiplayer mode back up, but it isn’t just what BioWare created in 2012, as the mod includes new ways of play, as well as some alterations to the multiplayer’s progression system. What started as a scheme to bring the old multiplayer mode back to the masses became much more as Mgamerz says the team got “too ambitious” and decided to add more on top of the old bones. This includes new modes Coalition, which pivots between different enemy factions across a match with each wave hosting a different group, and Unified, which mixes enemies from all groups together.

“it picks a quasi-random list of enemies,” Mgamerz says. “Originally we had it fully random, and that was terrible. It was so bad, but now it draws from all of the different wavelist options and picks from those, so you get like a mix of the original compositions that we made, and that one actually is pretty fun.”

One notable fact about the multiplayer mode is that it was one of the earliest examples of a game using modern-day loot boxes to let players unlock characters and acquire consumables. The progression system remains intact in the mod, but the ME3Tweaks modders wanted to give players options for whether they wanted to engage with the system or not.

“There’s going to be options for you to choose how much you want unlocked at the start,” Mgamerz says. “Do you want the full grind? If you want to do the full grind, nothing changes. Do you want to start with tons of credits? We can just edit the same file to give you 10 million credits so you can get a nice start. Maybe you want us to just give you one of everything. ‘Give me the level one of every item, so that I have everything I want, but I still have to level it.’ I want to respect people’s time because when I started playing this game, I had a lot of free time to play this game, and now that I’m much older, I would never dedicate the time to grinding everything out.”

The team is hoping to add a straightforward store for players to just buy things with credits directly, but Mgamerz says that won’t be available at launch.

“It’s definitely something we want to do, but it takes a new interface and hooking all of it up and testing it,” he says. “That takes several days, even with people that know how to edit interfaces better than me.”

The mod is also adding new items to the store, including new banners that feature images from the now-cancelled Mass Effect: Universe, so there’s more to unlock and see than there was in the base game, on top of playing and unlocking old stuff.

One advantage of the mod is that it’s not reliant on BioWare’s servers and allows players to make their own private servers to play with friends. In theory, that means it could outlast Mass Effect 3’s official multiplayer. When BioWare was shut down on October 21, the N7HQ website where players were able to track their multiplayer stats was seemingly also lost, which has fans concerned that ME3’s actual servers could follow sometime in the near future. For the ME3Tweaks mod group, this didn’t start out as a preservation project, but now that EA has closed BioWare, it has, perhaps involuntarily, become one, and Mgamerz says he has complicated feelings on that.

“When I had an interview for Pinnacle Station, people said the same thing,” he says. “They asked me, ‘Is this like game preservation to you?’ And I’m like, ‘Not in the sense that I think most people say, ‘Oh, well, you’re making it so that this stuff exists in the future,’ and it’s like no, I don’t know. It’s hard to explain how I feel on that front, but it’s going to continue. But I don’t feel like this replaces the original. I’m like, this is my rendition of it. BioWare’s will always be Bioware’s. Mine will build on BioWare’s, but I don’t ever want to feel like it replaces it because, you know, BioWare didn’t make it. I don’t feel like it’s fair to say that I built on top of your work and now my version replaces your work. That never feels right to me. But, I mean, once it goes away, this is the only way you’ll even be able to experience it.”

For the ME3Tweaks team, the notion of “replacing” BioWare’s work is daunting given the group had a long-standing relationship with the company.

“I went to work, and during the day, it was like it really started dawning on me,” Mgamerz says about the closure in October. “I’ve worked with this company on this company’s stuff for 14 years now. I’ve had an NDA with them for LE. I’ve toured their office in 2019. And so when it was gone, it was just kind of like this empty feeling. As a modder, and this is something I actually remember bringing up to EA in our call we had for Legendary. It was like, ‘you guys always recognize the artists. You guys always recognize the cosplayers and stuff, but it’s like you guys would literally never even acknowledge the modding scene, even though we did all this stuff.’ And they told us some stuff like, ‘Legendary Edition kind of came about because of the work you guys did. We saw what the modders were doing.’ I was on the team of the texture mod that they were pretty much citing. They’re like, “You’re the reason we kind of made this.’ right?” And so it was like, ’Oh, well, that’s quite a bit of acknowledgement.’ Yeah, that’s not public. And so you know, you build this hoping they would notice, even though you kind of know they never can really say anything.”

Mgamerz said that his contacts at BioWare were especially excited when the team brought Pinnacle Station to the Legendary Edition, which wasn’t included in the collection due to the source code being lost.

“They’d reach out and be like, ‘Oh my god, that’s crazy, dude. We never thought that’d be possible,’” he says.

As AI and vibe coding have become more prominent in the modding space, with some modders using AI to create assets and entire projects, Mgamerz says that the ME3Tweaks team didn’t use any AI to create or generate assets. However, he does say they used it in researching the actual game files. We’ve included his full explanation on the extent of AI use in the project below:

I don’t use it to make any assets, no visual, audio. I don’t use it for any of that kind of stuff. I have a tool I made that can research into the game files, but I literally made it so it never can save anything. It can only tell me like findings. So, it’s like, one of our kits for some reason just won’t appear, even though we are like 99 percent sure we’ve configured it properly. And it’ll go through, and I’ll be like, ‘Okay, well I looked at how the game code works, and I looked at how you define this kit, and there’s a flag that makes it only appear if it’s been unlocked with this entitlement, which is just like a number that we weren’t sure where it actually came from.’ And then, that reminded me, ‘Oh yeah, BioWare drip-fed old kits back in 2012 on one of their DLCs to pad it out a bit, and that was the flag that was causing this.’ And so, we would use AI to do stuff like that. You know, it can help us prototype some interface design outside of the game and get a feel of what actually works, what doesn’t. But I’ve been on the scene for so long. I know when I make a certain change, what kind of wider impact it has versus, now this isn’t me trying to talk down on [developers who vibe code] or anything. Vibe coding is one thing when you make something for yourself and you don’t release it because you’re the only person it affects, but when you release it and it becomes a wider product and it starts causing problems for other people, you’re not going to be the only one that has to deal with those problems. So, I don’t like when stuff like—We’re seeing a lot of vibe coded apps or mods now. The first sentence of the description is, ‘I didn’t write a single line of this; it was all done by Claude.’ And so, whenever it’s like reviewing scripts and helping me design stuff, I review every single line that gets output, and it’s so tedious, and I hate doing it. So I totally understand why vibe coding is a thing, but I understand that if I’m releasing this, I’m responsible for this, so I should be the one that reviews it. So if AI goes away, for whatever reason, there’s still some chance someone can understand what the heck this thing is doing.  And so I spend an awful lot of time going through all of the changes that we’re making.  We didn’t really use AI until the last probably four months. Almost everything before that was all done by hand.

With BioWare’s closure, Mgamerz and the ME3Tweaks team are now expecting “a lot more attention” than they were previously, as the mod is launching on N7 Day, a day when fans have celebrated the series’ legacy. Now, fans are more tuned in to the release than they might’ve been, as online and IRL demonstrations are underway and fans rally under the #SaveMassEffect banner. Some fans are even calling for people to play the Mass Effect: Legendary Edition on Steam for N7 Day in order to raise the collection’s concurrent player count.

“We’ve been doing this in secret, so it’s not going to be super…I mean, we’ve tested it, but I don’t know if it’s going to handle whatever influx of users it may get on the first day,” he says. “I’ve never run like a live-service kind of deal, even though it’s just like a server. So I’m like, yeah, going to be a stressful first day, probably for sure. But I’m sure it’ll even out. It always has. But yeah, I hope someone at BioWare—or former BioWare— notices.”

The Business Platinum Card® from American Express

As High As 300,000 points. Find Out Your Offer.

As High As 175,000 points. Find Out Your Offer.

Your Chase Ultimate Rewards card can help you earn bonus points in more ways than one — provided you remember an extra click before checkout.

Chase has an online shopping portal, called Shop and Earn, that lets eligible cardholders collect bonus Ultimate Rewards points when shopping at over 1,000 online retailers. Those bonus points stack with the points, miles or cash back you earn from your credit card, meaning the same purchases are doing double duty.

It's a small habit with a surprisingly satisfying payoff. Here's how to make sure those extra points actually land in your account.

Like other portals, Chase Shop and Earn is easy to use, but the order matters: Instead of beginning at a retailer's website, sign into your Ultimate Rewards account, find your preferred merchant within Shop and Earn and click through to its website.

You then shop and check out as usual, while Chase tracks the purchase and awards the advertised bonus points.

There are strong odds that you can earn on nearly any purchase, given the large number of participating websites. Advertised rates range from 1 to 15 bonus points per dollar spent, although participating stores and payouts change regularly.

These portal rewards are added on top of the points your Chase card normally earns. For example, if a retailer offers 5 bonus points per dollar spent through Shop and Earn, a qualifying $100 purchase would earn 500 bonus Ultimate Rewards points, plus the rewards earned from paying with your card. You may also be able to combine these earnings with Chase Offers for even more returns.

If you repeat this strategy every month, with a little extra shopping around the holidays, you could earn thousands of Ultimate Rewards each year, putting you closer toward your next award flight, hotel stay or other redemption.

The Shops at Chase is a separate offering for eligible Chase cardholders. While Shop and Earn acts as a shopping portal that awards bonus Ultimate Rewards points for qualifying online purchases, The Shops at Chase is an actual shopping marketplace. Through The Shops at Chase, cardholders can buy a curated selection of products using their Chase credit card and/or Ultimate Rewards points.

Related: Why I choose to earn Chase Ultimate Rewards points over Amex Membership Rewards points

Shop and Earn is available to cardholders who have at least one Chase credit card that's part of the Ultimate Rewards family. Some popular examples include:

Note that cobranded Chase airline and hotel cards aren't eligible for Shop and Earn.

If you're an eligible cardholder, there are several different pathways to access Shop and Earn from your online Chase account or mobile app. Personally, I find the easiest method is to go directly to ultimaterewardsearn.chase.com. There, you'll sign in to your account and select your eligible Chase account (if you have multiple qualifying cards).

You can also access Shop and Earn by navigating through the benefits sections of your online accounts or the Chase mobile app.

Offers sometimes vary by card, so it can be worth checking each of your eligible accounts before choosing a payout. Shopping aggregators like Cashback Monitor quickly display earning rates for different Chase cards as well as what you'd earn by using alternative shopping portals.

Aggregators are also a good way to determine if the Chase portal is the best option for your particular purchase. Sometimes, other portals offer a stronger return than Chase Shop and Earn, though this will depend somewhat on how you value Ultimate Rewards.

Related: Reasons to choose the Chase Sapphire Preferred over the fancier Chase Sapphire Reserve

Once you're on the Chase Shop and Earn website, you can search by merchant using the search box on the right, or you can scroll down to browse the stores currently offering elevated earning rates.

If you shop with a particular retailer regularly, click the heart icon to add it to your favorites and monitor its offers more easily.

When you're ready to shop, click on the relevant retailer's name. That will lead you to a screen outlining different earning rates by purchase type, as well as any additional terms and conditions for that specific merchant.

Pay attention to excluded products or brands, restrictions on coupon codes and other exclusions.

Related: How to redeem Chase Ultimate Rewards points for maximum value

Shopping portal bonuses rely on browser tracking, so a few small mistakes can prevent your purchase from qualifying.

If you close out of the window, there's no guarantee your visit will be tracked correctly for your rewards. When in doubt, you can always restart the process from Chase Shop and Earn.

You'll also want to pay close attention to terms and conditions. Chase doesn't normally award points on gift cards or purchases made with specialized coupon codes (in my experience, promo codes advertised directly on the merchant's website are usually allowed). Orders that are canceled, returned or otherwise refunded also won't qualify.

Chase says bonus points may appear as pending on your Shop and Earn shopping details page within 15 days, though in my experience, it often takes just a day or two. You should also receive an email confirming the purchase and the points earned.

Even when you can see the transaction was accurately tracked, points may take up to 60 days to fully post to your account. If they still haven't appeared by then, you can follow up with Chase via the phone number on the back of your card or through the online secure message center. You'll need the merchant name, purchase date, order total and ideally an e-receipt or confirmation email.

Points you earn with Shop and Earn typically appear as pending on your "Rewards Activity" page within a few days, though it can take up to two billing cycles for your bonus points to post.

If you're shopping online without checking Chase Shop and Earn, you could be leaving free travel on the table. Returns go further than simple cash back, and the math is simple: Even small transactions snowball into larger balances that can lead you to some serious redemption power.

‘I’ve Built Stronger Arms in My 40s Than I Had in My 20s—These Are the 3 Exercises I Never Skip’

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Perimenopause strength coach Chrissa Benson says these three dumbbell exercises have helped her build stronger, more muscular arms than she had two decades ago.

Building (and maintaining) upper-body strength as we age is crucial. As well as supporting everyday, functional movements such as lifting, pushing, pulling and reaching, having strong arms can help us get back up again if we were to fall—ultimately meaning we can remain independent for longer.

Sarcopenia (age-related muscle loss) can begin to affect us as early as the age of 30. From then, research suggests that muscle mass decreases by approximately 3-8% per decade—with the rate of decline speeding up after 60.

But the good news is that we aren’t powerless in slowing that decline. There’s lots of things we can do, with strength training being one of the most important.

As well as building, well, strength, such training supports other areas of health, too—with one recent study associating two hours of strength work a week with a 22% lower risk of developing major cardiovascular disease and a 44% lower risk of having a heart attack.

Someone who is well-acquainted with the benefits of strength training is certified perimenopause strength coach and fitness content creator Chrissa Benson, who says she has more muscle now in her 40s than she ever did in her 20s.

And recently, on Instagram, she shared three arm exercises that have helped her build some of that muscle. In fact, Benson added that she now never skips them.

“Underrated for targeting the back and shoulders. Go lighter on this one, I’m using 10lbs here,” says Benson.

“Gives a standard bicep curl a serious run for its money. I go a little heavier here, 15lbs, nice and slow and controlled.”

“Never skipping these. Heavy set, every time. So good for the chest and back.”

Hannah Bradfield is a Senior Health and Fitness Writer for Women’s Health UK. An NCTJ-accredited journalist, Hannah graduated from Loughborough University with a BA in English and Sport Science and an MA in Media and Cultural Analysis.  She has been covering sports, health and fitness for the last five years and has created content for outlets including BBC Sport, BBC Sounds, Runner’s World and Stylist. She especially enjoys interviewing those working within the community to improve access to sport, exercise and wellness. Hannah is a 2024 John Schofield Trust Fellow and was also named a 2022 Rising Star in Journalism by The Printing Charity.  A keen runner, Hannah was firmly a sprinter growing up (also dabbling in long jump) but has since transitioned to longer-distance running. While 10K is her favoured race distance, she loves running or volunteering at parkrun every Saturday, followed, of course, by pastries. She’s always looking for fun new runs and races to do and brunch spots to try.

How This Busy Dad Got Leaner and Stronger in 11 Weeks Without Losing Much Weight

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Strength coach Gareth Sapstead shares how he helped a busy dad and business owner get leaner, build muscle, and reshape his physique.

Body recomposition is widely viewed as the holy grail of physical transformation: losing body fat while simultaneously adding lean muscle.

It’s also often dismissed as either impossible or reserved exclusively for complete beginners. The logic appears fairly convincing. To lose fat, you need to consume fewer calories than you burn. To build muscle, you need enough energy to construct new tissue. One requires a deficit, the other seemingly demands a surplus.

Pick a lane: bulk first, cut later. Right?

Strength and physique coach Gareth Sapstead disagrees. In a detailed breakdown published on his website, Sapstead explains how he helped father and business owner Mark Schadenberg transform his physique ahead of a Men’s Health Netherlands photoshoot—losing fat, adding visible muscle and finishing just 4 pounds lighter after 11 weeks.

The scales barely budged, but his body changed dramatically.

“The fitness industry has been telling you to pick one for decades,” says Sapstead. “Bulk first, cut later. Build the muscle, then reveal it.”

Sapstead accepts that fat loss requires an energy deficit, while building muscle requires energy. However, he says the apparent contradiction comes from treating your body like a single energy account.

“Fat tissue and muscle tissue operate through different pathways,” he explains. “You can be breaking down stored fat for fuel while simultaneously building muscle protein—as long as the conditions for muscle protein synthesis are met.”

Those conditions are relatively straightforward: enough protein and a sufficiently challenging resistance-training stimulus.

Sapstead points towards research examining simultaneous fat loss and muscle gain. According to Sapstead, the phenomenon appeared consistently across the research and wasn’t simply a beginner-only fluke.

He also references studies in which participants consumed between 400 and 800 additional calories each day, all from protein.

By conventional logic, those extra calories should have resulted in weight gain. Instead, Sapstead says the participants either maintained their weight or lost fat while gaining or preserving muscle.

“Body recomposition is real,” he concludes. “It’s well-supported, and for the right person in the right circumstances, it happens faster than most expect.”

Schadenberg had just 11 weeks to prepare for his photoshoot. He was running a business and raising a family—a familiar combination for many of us—that had gradually pushed training, nutrition, and recovery down his list of priorities.

“He’d let his training slip,” says Sapstead. “Not from a lack of drive, but through the entirely predictable reality of running a business and raising a family.”

His sleep was suffering, his diet lacked structure and he had no consistent exercise routine. Like many men in their late 30s and 40s, Sapstead says, he was also “chronically undereating protein.”

That might not sound like an ideal starting point, but it gave Schadenberg several powerful variables to improve almost immediately.

“Every one of those factors is a recomposition accelerant,” says Sapstead.

Returning to resistance training after a significant lay-off can produce a response closer to that of a beginner. The stimulus feels novel again, adaptations arrive quickly and previously built muscle may return faster than it took to develop in the first place.

Combine that with more protein, better recovery and structured training, and you can create the conditions to rebuild muscle while using stored body fat to help cover the energy costs.

“The body has been starved of the right inputs,” says Sapstead. “Give it protein, training and adequate recovery, and it responds.”

After 11 weeks, Schadenberg was only 4 pounds lighter. But his before-and-after photographs showed considerably more muscle and significantly less body fat.

“Fat came off. Muscle went on. Bodyweight barely moved,” says Sapstead.

Sapstead says recomposition is particularly well suited to anyone who has been away from consistent strength training, is carrying more body fat than they would like, habitually eats too little protein or has allowed sleep and recovery to deteriorate.

For experienced lifters who have trained intelligently, eaten well, and slept properly for years, the process is likely to be slower. There’s simply less low-hanging fruit.

For returning lifters, however, they're in a prime position for a recomp.

This isn’t the time for an aggressive diet or an uncontrolled bulk. Sapstead recommends eating around your maintenance calories or slightly above them, with approximately 200 to 300 additional calories coming predominantly from protein.

The aim is to provide enough energy to support training and muscle growth without creating a large surplus that encourages unnecessary fat gain.

Sapstead sets protein at around one gram per pound of bodyweight as a starting point, although higher intakes may also be appropriate.

Protein provides the raw materials required to repair and construct muscle tissue. It is also highly satiating, making it easier to control your overall food intake without constantly feeling hungry.

Perform three to four structured resistance-training sessions each week, with a focus on progressive overload.

Your body needs a reason to preserve and build muscle. That means gradually adding weight, completing more reps, improving your range of motion or producing better-quality work—not simply getting hot and tired doing random circuits, or going through the motions with zero intensity.

When fat is coming off while muscle is being added, your bodyweight may barely change. That doesn’t mean the approach has stopped working. It may mean it’s working exactly as intended.

“The scale would have told him nothing was happening,” Sapstead says of Schadenberg’s transformation. “The photos would have told him everything.”

Take progress pictures every two to three weeks, track your gym performance and pay attention to how your clothes fit. If your waist is shrinking, your strength is improving and you look more muscular, a stable bodyweight isn’t failure—it’s the holy grail of body recomp in action.

With almost 18 years in the health and fitness space as a personal trainer, nutritionist, breath coach and writer, Andrew has spent nearly half of his life exploring how to help people improve their bodies and minds.

As our fitness editor he prides himself on keeping Men’s Health at the forefront of reliable, relatable and credible fitness information, whether that’s through writing and testing thousands of workouts each year, taking deep dives into the science behind muscle building and fat loss or exploring the psychology of performance and recovery.

Whilst constantly updating his knowledge base with seminars and courses, Andrew is a lover of the practical as much as the theory and regularly puts his training to the test tackling everything from Crossfit and strongman competitions, to ultra marathons, to multiple 24 hour workout stints and (extremely unofficial) world record attempts.

You can find Andrew on Instagram at @theandrew.tracey, or simply hold up a sign for ‘free pizza’ and wait for him to appear.

Tectonic forces near Japan helped fuel the formation of the Grand Canyon

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

A double subduction zone off the coast of Japan caused rifting halfway around the world to accelerate, fueling the formation of some of North America's most iconic features.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

A geological reorganization near Japan may have led to the formation of one of North America's most recognizable features.

The birth of the Gulf of California, which rifted from Mexico and created the Baja Peninsula 6 million to 8 million years ago, was accelerated by the formation of a double subduction zone more than 6,000 miles (9,600 kilometers) away, near Japan. This reorganization led to a transfer of tectonic force across the Pacific tectonic plate, which, in turn, opened the Gulf of California and ultimately led to the Grand Canyon's formation, according to new research published Oct. 29 in the journal Science.

The findings will help researchers understand more about plate boundaries, which is where most of the strongest earthquakes on the planet originate.

"It's more than a regional curiosity," study co-author Thorsten Becker, a geophysicist at the University of Texas at Austin (UT Austin), told Live Science. "It's a reminder of the global connectedness of mantle convection and plate tectonics."

The Gulf of California is an example of a rift zone, a place where continental crust pulls apart and creates a spot so low that it can be filled by ocean water. There are many rift zones around the world; a currently active one is the Red Sea. But rifting can slow down, speed up or peter out entirely. In the Gulf of California, rifting not only persisted, but sped up. Rifting took about 6 million to 13 million years ‪—‬ much quicker than the typical speed of 30 million to 80 million years.

Study co-author Guido Gianni, a geophysicist at the Czech Academy of Sciences, wondered why that was. No previous research fully explained the rapid rifting, which changed the face of North America in many ways. Not only did the rifting transfer Baja California to the Pacific Plate, but it provided an outlet for the developing Colorado River, which subsequently carved the Grand Canyon on its way to sea level.

Gianni and Becker's doctoral student at UT Austin, Xiang He, used a global model of plate tectonics, run on supercomputers at the Texas Advanced Computing Center on campus, to find out if far-flung forces might provide an explanation. Around the same time the Gulf of California was pulling apart, Japan was undergoing its own geological changes. Off the coast of Japan, the Pacific Plate subducts, or pushes under, the Eurasian Plate. About 10 million to 12 million years ago, a single subduction zone near Japan transitioned into two separate subduction zones.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

A double subduction zone off Japan (left inset) accelerated the growth of the Gulf of California (right inset).

This transition at the western end of the Pacific Plate influenced the tectonic forces on the other side of the plate, He, Gianni, Becker and their colleagues found.

"It turned out — ta-da! — [with] the change in this pulling stress, Baja California was one of the few places outside the Japan region that were affected," Becker said. It was "unexpected and sort of a little bit too good to be true."

The researchers tweaked their model to see if the result held with different assumptions about stresses and mantle properties, and it did.

influenced the tectonic forces on the other side of the plate, He, Gianni, Becker and their colleagues found.

"It turned out — ta-da! — [with] the change in this pulling stress, Baja California was one of the few places outside the Japan region that were affected," Becker said. It was "unexpected and sort of a little bit too good to be true."

The researchers tweaked their model to see if the result held with different assumptions about stresses and mantle properties, and it did.

The Gulf of California was already rifting regardless of the situation across the Pacific. The initial rift was caused by the subduction of a tectonic plate called the Farallon Plate, located under the West Coast of North America. The Farallon slab stretched and thinned the continental crust of the North American plate as it moved. But such rifting might have ended with nothing more than the formation of a little valley or low spot if Japan's influence had not kicked in to give the rifting some extra oomph.

The findings have a direct effect on scientists' understanding of how plate boundaries form, Becker said. And this isn't just academic. The findings could have implications for understanding earthquake-generating faults such as the San Andreas in California and the Cascadia subduction zone in the Pacific Northwest, Becker said. Cascadia is what's known as a "megathrust" boundary, capable of triggering quakes of more than magnitude 9.

"If I'm asking what is the mantle doing underneath Cascadia so I get our megathrust correct," Becker said, "I need to include these effects."

Gianni, G. M., He, X., Becker, T. W., & Faccenna, C. (2026). Western Pacific double subduction drives Gulf of California rifting. Science, 394(6823), 515–519. https://doi.org/10.1126/science.aef5187

Stephanie Pappas is a contributing writer for Live Science, covering topics ranging from geoscience to archaeology to the human brain and behavior. She was previously a senior writer for Live Science but is now a freelancer based in Denver, Colorado, and regularly contributes to Scientific American and The Monitor, the monthly magazine of the American Psychological Association. Stephanie received a bachelor's degree in psychology from the University of South Carolina and a graduate certificate in science communication from the University of California, Santa Cruz.

What's the toughest choice you've made in an RPG?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Welcome to Dungeon Master, PC Gamer's regular RPG column, where Online Editor Fraser Brown delves into PC gaming's most beloved and enduring genre. Grab a seat in our badly-lit tavern and please ignore the goblin puke.

We love a big RPG dilemma, don't we? Right up until the point where we actually have to make the choice and we stare at our screens for an uncomfortably long time, hoping we'll figure out a way to make a call and not feel absolutely terrible about it later.

For my money, the best—agonising, guilt-inducing—choices are the really personal ones. I love that Mass Effect lets me determine the fate of the galaxy in all these different ways, but it never gives me a reason to care about the rachni, for instance, or the council, so when I have to decide to save them or let them die, it becomes more of an abstract moral choice.

Disco Elysium, then, left me in tatters. It's more personal and intimate than a productive therapy session. There's a card you can read pretty early on, stuck inside a ledger, that opens the wound that Harry worked very hard—by abusing alcohol and drugs—to forget. If you toss it, you never get it back. That was a hard one. Is ignoring it a kind of liberation? Or are you just helping Harry hide from his trauma?

It's effective because it's powerfully relatable. You don't even need to have experienced a cataclysmic breakup to understand Harry in that moment. We've all got shit we'd really love to forget. Self-destructive choices that we made in a heated moment, leaving regret to constantly claw at us.

Relationship drama should be excellent fodder for RPG choices, but that rarely ends up being true. Usually they end up being friction free, but sometimes you'll be walloped with the classic trope of being forced to decide if they live or die—a cheap trick to force us to care about someone, even if they actually suck. Yeah, I'm talking about you, Ash.

That's why Geralt's romantic misadventures are so effective at spitting out challenging conundrums. The best thing about The Witcher 3's approach is that you can choose not to make a decision. It's so rare for games to recognise that indecision is another choice.

I desperately wanted Geralt to be happy. He deserved it. But I could not figure out the best way to go about giving him his happily ever after. The depths of his feelings for Yennefer nudge you towards making sure they have a life together, but god she's toxic. She regularly makes him feel like shit. Triss actually seems to make him happy, but is that just the safe pick?

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

So I never made the choice. I told both of them I loved them, and they left me tied to a bed while they went off to get drunk. My inability to make a choice was the worst choice. But it also ended up feeling poignant—Geralt so often gets in the way of his own happiness.

OK, what about you lot? What's the toughest choice I've had to make in an RPG? Let me know in the comments.

Samsung Wallet’s USDC on Solana Is Live — Here’s What the Rollout Actually Covers

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: A Solana Foundation update dated November 5 confirms that Samsung Wallet’s stablecoin account went live on November 2 for eligible US Galaxy owners. USDC transfers and certain international bank payouts are supported, but the headline 82 million compatible devices should not be mistaken for 82 million active users.

A crypto feature that does not feel like a crypto feature may be one of the most consequential ways digital payments reach a wider audience. In a November 5 launch confirmation, the Solana Foundation said Samsung Wallet’s Stablecoin Account became available on November 2. For eligible US Galaxy users, it places USDC inside a familiar wallet app rather than asking them to install and learn a separate cryptocurrency application.

That is a tangible step beyond the launch window described in NewsBTC’s earlier look at Samsung’s planned rollout. But the most useful questions concern what people can do today, who qualifies and what still depends on outside providers.

The Foundation says eligible users can buy, hold, send and receive USDC over Solana within Samsung Wallet. Bastion provides licensed custody and infrastructure, with Coinbase serving as sub-custodian through Coinbase Prime Vault. That structure matters. Customers see the convenience of a familiar interface, while their assets still depend on a chain of infrastructure and custody providers.

US customers can transfer USDC to other eligible Samsung Wallet users and compatible external wallets. The announcement says Samsung charges no transfer fee for these routes, although other providers may charge fees. A separate path allows eligible users to send funds to qualifying bank accounts in more than 60 countries, potentially arriving as local currency. Available corridors and charges vary by destination; the distinction between a blockchain transfer and a bank payout is important.

The feature sits inside Samsung Wallet’s Quick access area alongside payment cards and everyday credentials. That small interface decision could matter more to adoption than a headline transaction-speed benchmark, because habitual use often depends on how much friction an app removes.

The announcement refers to approximately 82 million compatible Galaxy devices in the United States. That is a ceiling for potential access, not evidence of adoption. It says nothing about the number of activated accounts, transfers completed, average transaction size or users who return the following month.

The eligibility restrictions are also substantial. According to the Foundation, users need to be at least 18, reside in the US, have a Samsung account and use a compatible Galaxy device running Android 13 or later. New York residents are excluded at launch. These requirements make the real reachable population smaller than the device figure.

The payments story extends the institutional work covered in NewsBTC’s report on Solana’s delivery-versus-payment tool, but the two solve different problems. One targets consumer remittances; the other aims at financial-asset settlement.

Samsung has described potential future expansion into online and in-store stablecoin payments and additional markets, subject to regulation. Those ideas are not live features simply because the stablecoin account has launched. Nor should this launch be read as a forecast for SOL’s price: consumer payments can be valuable without creating a predictable relationship between wallet activity and demand for the network token.

A fair assessment will require active-account numbers, real-world fees, payout times, failed-payment handling and whether the feature reduces friction relative to existing remittance services. The verified news is that the service is now operational for a defined group of customers. The commercial test begins after that announcement, when people decide whether to use it again.

This article was written by the News Desk and edited by Samuel Rae.

"I feel that sense of responsibility more acutely": After BioWare's closure, the makers of a new Mass Effect 3 co-op remaster mod look to carry their legacy

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

"It's like watching a friend lose a parent - maybe you're not attached, but you feel the secondhand distress"

It appears I was premature in suggesting that the closure of BioWare might quickly spell an end for Mass Effect 3's strangely good, preposterously enduring co-op mode. I reinstalled and logged on just last week, and easily found a series of packed lobbies. Still, it was always regrettable that 2021's Mass Effect Legendary Edition opted not to remaster this multiplayer component alongside its dolled-up main trilogy, thus failing to introduce to a new generation the joys of performing teleporting psychic headbutts as a millennia-old bipedal frog.

This oversight has now been addressed, with the release of the Legendary Edition Multiplayer mod for said remaster. It's both a reconstruction and a slight expansion of ME3 multi, built in secret since 2023 by the ME3Tweaks group: a modding collective who've previously imbued the Legendary Edition with Mass Effect 1's Pinnacle Station DLC. It's also, some of its makers tell me, a bittersweet expression of appreciation for a series that looks unlikely to continue after EA's Mass Effect 5 cancellation.

At least some of that affection can be seen in how Legendary Edition Multiplayer differs from the already-available LEMP mod, which uses AI vibecoding to slap a mechanically identical recreation of ME3's co-op into the Legendary Edition's ones and zeroes. ME3Tweaks's version instead adds, according to member and longtime ME modder Audemus, a "completely overhauled UI, rebaked level lighting, upscaled and redrawn textures, upgraded shaders, native LE features such as HDR and controller support, networking and server replication improvements, extensive bug fixes, and entirely new wave modes such as Unified and Coalition."

Unified mode, for instance, quite literally mixes up ME3's enemy factions by forming mishmashed waves of baddies from across the Geth, Reaper, and Cerberus armies, thereby dusting off and stamping on your 14-year-old battle tactics. The mod also offers the ability to use ME3Tweaks' own servers for matchmaking, though a private server option remains for scraps with mates.

I'll definitely be giving this a try, once I'm back from my current travels. Though as with those OG ME3 multiplayer sessions last week, it'll be with a certain glumness, knowing that – despite a vocal campaign to give Mass Effect 5 a reprieve– I'll be playing something that had its future cut short.

The circumstances aren't lost on Legendary Edition Multiplayer's creators. "BioWare's closure is an incredibly sad moment for the games industry, and a particularly heartbreaking thing for the Mass Effect fanbase to contend with," Audemus tells me over email. "I feel as though our responsibility as a modding community has always been to carry the torch for this franchise, with or without an official future for it. But now that the future of Mass Effect has never been more in question, I feel that sense of responsibility more acutely than I ever have before."

Mgamerz, another modder on the project, recalls how "The day BioWare closed it didn't hit me until later in the day, a sort of numbness I can't really describe. I've spent 14 years working on their games - it's like watching a friend lose a parent - maybe you're not attached, but you feel the secondhand distress."

For some Legendary Edition Multiplayer modders, the affected BioWare staff included developers they knew personally. ME3Tweaks had previously been invited to embargoed discussions with both BioWare and EA on game modding, and Mass Effect writer and director Mac Walters (who left BioWare voluntarily in 2023) allegedly advocated for an even closer relationship.

"It's a shame to see it shut down," Mgamerz says. "Everyone speculated about it, but I know from talking to people internally there, that nobody knew the guillotine was about to drop." [Owners EA say the decision was only made after "months of planning, review, and analysis", though independent reporting suggests it was largely down to a straight choice between making ME5 or Star Wars Battlefront 3.]

"When we had our NDA with BioWare (since expired), apparently Mac Walters pushed internally to get my dev team brought into the manifold," he adds. "I say this as 'apparently', cause due to scheduling issues we never got to speak with Mac. But secondhand stories tell me he was a big proponent of modders - something that I would have loved to see more publicly shown.

"It is disappointing to know that the people who helped build the world my work now builds upon were killed unceremoniously - a problem that plagues the industry, and what kept me from further pursuing entry into it, something I used to dream of doing when I was younger."

RPS lawyers are telling me to tell you that nobody was literally killed. All the same, and even as someone much more familiar with playing the Mass Effects than tinkering around in their engine bays, the ME3Tweaks team make relatable points: we can frolick around an uprezzed Firebase Hydro, biotically biffing half-robot Turians like it's 2012, but it'll be hard not to look at all those conspicuous waist-high walls and think about the people who put them there. The same's true of having your mind drift to thoughts of their successors.

But there's another way of looking at it, which is that mods like Legendary Edition Multiplayer can preserve and maintain an original developer's work well beyond what their leveraged-buyout executive overlords might deem financially inconvenient. "I hope," as Audemus puts it, "that this mod - along with the fact that we've been able to build the technology to make mods like this in the first place - sends a message that there is incredible value in this franchise, and a passionate fanbase that is not ready to say goodbye to this universe and these games."

Remove your personal info from the web (and stop it coming back)

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

It seems incredible that anyone can legally create a business which involves scraping your personal data from the web and then selling it, yet here we are. There are literally hundreds of data brokers doing just this.

They also buy data from app developers, who use innocuous-sounding terms like “sharing data with selected partners” in their privacy polices. Fortunately, individuals and families can fight back this Cybersecurity Month.

Personal data bought and sold includes everything from your web-browsing habits and interests to your location history. The least-worst case is that your personal data is purchased by companies wanting to target you with ads. In the worst case, hackers gain access to that data, where it is made available to scammers and identity thieves.

You can fight back using what’s known as “takedown requests.” By law, data brokers have to remove your data if you tell them to. However, this is much easier said than done.

First, you would have to contact literally hundreds of different companies. Second, many of those companies deliberately make takedown links hard to find, then force you to jump through all sorts of hoops in order to issue the requests. Some of them even break the law by not providing the links in the first place.

But even if you succeed, the results won’t last for long. Data brokers are constantly buying new data and adding it to your profile. It’s a constant game of whack-a-mole.

That’s where Incogni comes in. Not only do they do all of the hard work for you across literally hundreds of data brokers, genealogy websites, and social media, but they monitor to ensure that it has been done, scan for the later addition of new data, and issue new takedown requests for that.

Unlike other services, Incogni helps remove your sensitive information from all broker types, including those tricky People Search Sites that are often missed.

With the company’s Unlimited plan, you can even send links you’ve discovered yourself and Incogni will take care of it for you.

As for the latest team news, De Rossi has a much better situation than Amorim with no injuries or suspensions to note. Lorenzo Venturino and Elias Havel were both on the sidelines but have returned for this one.

It looks like the former Milan men El Shaarawy and Colombo will start, while Junior Messias could perhaps be introduced from the bench. De Rossi didn’t give much away during his presser in terms of the team selection, but did make sure to express his respect for the Rossoneri.
