Talking Point: What Made The Xbox 360 Era So Incredible In Your Opinion?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

As we continue celebrating the past 25 years of Xbox throughout the course of the month, we thought we'd use this opportunity to look at the Xbox 360 - a console that's consistently rated as the best system in the history of the brand.

The Xbox 360 will be turning 21 years old on November 22nd, and its impact is still being felt today. This is the console that gave us such features as Party Chat, modern-style Xbox dashboards, the ability to download mainstream games digitally, and a ridiculous number of incredible franchises that have won over the hearts and minds of many.

For what it's worth, we did actually talk about some of this stuff for the 20th anniversary last year:

Feature Seven Things We Miss About The Xbox 360 Era In 2025

As we rapidly approach its 20th anniversary

With that said though, every era of Xbox has treated us to innovative new features and amazing games that have stood the test of time. The PS3 existed at the same time as the Xbox 360, and yet somehow, Microsoft's console felt like the more popular of the two, despite selling slightly less units overall in the end.

It even managed to navigate a devastating technical issue in the form of the Red Ring of Death, and somehow still convince the masses that it was one of the greatest consoles of all time. That's some feat.

So, what made the Xbox 360 era so incredible in your opinion? Was it just a brilliantly-designed system, RROD aside? Did it innovate in ways that no other system did? Were the games simply that good? Or has its popularity become overblown since the horribly botched launch of the Xbox One in 2013?

Let us know your thoughts on why the Xbox 360 era was so good down in the comments section below.

Exclusive Xbox 360 Fan Talks To Us About Completing Their Collection After 20 Years

"My mission is to get achievements in every single game..."

Fraser is the News Editor at Pure Xbox, where he spends his time reporting on the biggest stories in the world of Xbox and beyond.

It had all the best titles, plenty of exclusives and the broadest content. It was an exciting platform

What made it special was creative games, exclusives and visual gap: innovative titles for their time, featuring beautiful HD 3D graphics and smooth gameplay. It was also the era of creative games and iconic franchises like Gears of War, Mass Effect, Dead Space, and BioShock.

I feel like this is the formula that Asha is trying to recreate with Helix: a return to exclusive games and a revolutionary technical platform. What's still missing is the creativity aspect, and that's where I still have some doubts, just as I do about the industry as a whole. Risk-taking has become minimal, and we're being flooded with remasters and sequels.

That said, considering the enormous cost of game development today, I can understand why risks have to be carefully calculated. Even so, Xbox has been by far the most creative platform of this console generation compared to its competitors, so hopefully that continues.

some of the best single player games ever made came out this generation.

and for multiplayer xbox live was fantastic

Amazing games and new ip and exclusives. The console and the games really lead the way for me. The list is endless and we all know them.

The first half of the 360 generation was peak xbox in my opinion and great until Kinect came and changed everything.

The games, leadership team at the time and the console itself were great. I was one of the lucky few that never had RRoD either!

Many great exclusives and console was best

In my opinion, it was a combination of factors.

1. Exclusive titles: Dead Rising, Lost Odyssey, Oblivion, Mass Effect, Gears of War, Tales of Vesperia... It was a nonstop stream of great games that gave the platform its identity.

2. The controller: My favorite at the time was the GameCube controller, but as soon as I tried the Xbox 360 controller, I realized they’d achieved perfect ergonomics.

3. The achievements system: I loved the idea of having a profile where all my achievements were tracked under a single score.

4. The technical leap: Seeing PGR3 in 2005 was an incredible experience, as was experiencing HD for the first time.

The exclusives, the achievements, the superior versions of multiplats over ps3, the controllers, titles such as the original dead rising, elder scrolls oblivion released first on the console, halo 3 multiplayer, left 4 dead 1&2,1vs 100..the lost goes on

Honestly, I don't believe it was all that Incredible. The Xbox 360 was a competent console that provided a solid user experience with a handful of notable exclusives, a handful more solid multiplatform titles, and it brought mainstream accessibility to HD gaming.

Beyond that, though, it didn't really do much of anything that the original Xbox hadn't already done before it and the OG Xbox had a vastly superior library of exclusives and noteworthy multiplats and did far more to push the boundaries of innovation with it's Xbox Live service, Dual Analog controllers, and the inclusion of an internal harddrive. Even the arrival of the 360's Arcade marketplace was something that was initially tested with the original Xbox.

Don't get me wrong, the 360 was a good console, at least in the first half of its lifespan, but the original Xbox was the workhorse that made it all possible and I find myself pining to play games like Crimson Skies, Mechassault, KOTOR, and Jade Empire far more often than I do anything that came out during the 360 generation.

these are the first games I owned. oblivion, mass effect, dragon age, fallout 3. look at that list those are all time bangers it was just fun to play on.

Oh, man, EVERYTHING!!!! GREATEST CONSOLE OF ALL TIME 🙏😭🙏😭

I’m probably forgetting more, but man the 360 was amazing.

Because they mimicked Playstation’s game. Lots of great third party games, exclusives and AAA timed exclusives. No exclusives day 1 on a sub service. It works for Playstation, it works for Nintendo. Since the 360 it’s been bad dicision after bad dicision.

Call of Duty 4: Modern Warfare was marketed by MS to sell Gold and that helped drive the popularity of Online gaming and Call of Duty too .

It had amazing games and amazing exclusives, simple as that. And there will never be an Xbox like it again with the way Microsoft and other companies do things now.

For me it really just comes down to games. I've got and have played quite a bit more 360 games than Xbox One games, to an order of, like, three time as much, I think.

That said I also didn't have an original Xbox and don't have a Series X, so my experience for this question is limited to two generations.

The exclusives games that you need to have an xbox 360 to play only and of course that Microsoft was really cared. They were stable they didnt change opinion or strategy every one year. They cared bro... For real... Or at least they were not greedy for a while.

It had great third party exclusives such as GTA IV, Assassin's Creed II, Madden and compared to the PS3 anyone could afford it. It was a nice companion console to the Wii. Then you added its online features and digital storefront it was just a solid console all around.

If I had to recommend a console for someone without a lot of money, it would still be a 360. They will have enough to play for a lifetime. This is also why the Series/One are good, because Xbox did a great job with backwards compatibility with most of these games being playable.

The games aswell as the PS3 Slim, despite the whole generation basically being the brown generation due to Unreal 3.

It was at a good time where production cycles weren't super long and they still had the fun of the 90s and 2000s baked into some silliness even in serious games. Swapping faceplates was neat though didn't do it. It was a good combination of Xbox franchises hitting their strides and having lots of fun great content. Nice question to ask and very positive feelings back then that held over to Xbox One which was not great and even to Series X. This is an easy question to answer: everything!! The console was great and the games were incredible. Discogs’ Kevin Lewandowski can’t live without his AeroPress

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The founder of Discogs talks coffee, Rez, and (of course) records.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

The founder of Discogs talks coffee, Rez, and (of course) records.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Twenty-five years ago, Kevin Lewandowski founded Discogs, arguably the destination online for obsessive vinyl collectors and music nerds of all stripes. It is a marketplace for buying and selling records, obviously, but it’s also become so much more. It’s a shockingly comprehensive database that covers not just albums and singles, but all the various versions and pressings. It’s a really fun rabbit hole to disappear down when you’re trying to figure out what obscure pressing of Ministry’s The Mind is a Terrible Thing to Taste or Psalm 69 you had in high school, because you definitely blasted the cover of “Lay Lady Lay” all the time and 100 percent never owned Filth Pig.

In the years since it debuted, Discogs has grown to be much more than just a site where audiophiles go to trade rare first pressings of Beach Boys records. There are forums, groups, user-created lists, and more. It’s part eBay, part Reddit, and IMDB — just specifically for music.

With more and more people turning their back on the algorithm and rediscovering physical media, now seemed like a perfect time to check in with Lewandowski.

My AeroPress. I use it multiple times a day. In fact, I just made a coffee with it [sips coffee].

How many tabs do you have open right now?

Probably around 30 on my work computer. My personal computer is worse — probably 40 or 50.

Which social media platform do you use the most?

I look at X and Instagram occasionally, but I don’t really post.

What is your favorite gadget you’ve ever owned?

It’s not really a gadget, but I like to tinker. Lately I’ve been playing around with Home Assistant software and built my own setup to control things like the garage door and outdoor lights. So I guess my favorite gadget is one I made myself.

What is one thing you wish you had created?

Probably a game like Rez, which came out around 2000. It’s a rhythm game where you’re almost controlling the music as you play, about as close to DJing as a video game can get. I’ve actually had an idea for my own version with a similar concept. It’s still just an idea, though.

I’ll either clear everything off my desk or change locations completely. Go to a coffee shop or sit outside. Sometimes I just need a different environment.

What’s the last piece of physical media you bought?

Records. I was at Mint Gallery in Portland last week and picked up four. I still really like going into a record store and finding something to bring home. And I also recently picked up three reissues from the Moodsville / Acoustic Sounds series.

What would the tagline for your biopic be?

I don’t use memes very often, but one I was kind of proud of was putting a Discogs screen into a North Korean war room photo. I was advised that maybe it wasn’t one for the company chat.

While some retailers were offering preorder discounts on the remastered Legend of Zelda: The Ocarina of Time, it’s not too late to save if you missed out. Costco members can currently get $5 off the digital version online, bringing the price down to $54.99. Considering an annual Costco membership runs $65, that discount almost offsets one month of your plan — a prime example of “gamer math.”

My colleague Andrew Webster recently shared his thoughts on Nintendo’s latest Switch 2 exclusive, noting how impressed he was with how much impact a few modern quality-of-life changes had on what is otherwise a classic experience. The updated graphics are as thoughtful as they are beautiful, pairing realistic scenery with puppet-like characters. Perhaps, more importantly, Link can now jump anytime he likes, rather than being limited to specific ledges.
